# English Form 4, Form Four English: authored answers for review

Every answer fixed by a person, not computed. Part 1: the tables, conventions and rules that computed answers come from.
Part 2: every authored question template, with all its data (keys, statements, pairs, choices and feedback) and sources.
Regenerate with `npm run review:f4-english`. Item IDs (TR-…) match `docs/teacher-review.md`.

## Part 1

Rules the computed answers use are listed in the subject’s library tests; every authored answer is in Part 2.

### `e4-2-terms` · Lesson 2 · multiple choice, level 1

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Which term means a courteous proposal of assistance that leaves a choice? | polite offer | clarifying question · respectful language · appropriate referral | That concerns a question establishing what support is actually needed, rather than a courteous proposal of assistance that leaves a choice. · That concerns wording that preserves a person’s dignity, rather than a courteous proposal of assistance that leaves a choice. · That concerns seeking a responsible adult or trained support for a concern beyond the learner’s role, rather than a courteous proposal of assistance that leaves a choice. | The person should be able to accept or decline. |
| Which term means a question establishing what support is actually needed? | clarifying question | polite offer · respectful language · appropriate referral | That concerns a courteous proposal of assistance that leaves a choice, rather than a question establishing what support is actually needed. · That concerns wording that preserves a person’s dignity, rather than a question establishing what support is actually needed. · That concerns seeking a responsible adult or trained support for a concern beyond the learner’s role, rather than a question establishing what support is actually needed. | Ask about the particular need rather than assuming it. |
| Which term means wording that preserves a person’s dignity? | respectful language | polite offer · clarifying question · appropriate referral | That concerns a courteous proposal of assistance that leaves a choice, rather than wording that preserves a person’s dignity. · That concerns a question establishing what support is actually needed, rather than wording that preserves a person’s dignity. · That concerns seeking a responsible adult or trained support for a concern beyond the learner’s role, rather than wording that preserves a person’s dignity. | A relevant question can be considerate and clear. |
| Which term means seeking a responsible adult or trained support for a concern beyond the learner’s role? | appropriate referral | polite offer · clarifying question · respectful language | That concerns a courteous proposal of assistance that leaves a choice, rather than seeking a responsible adult or trained support for a concern beyond the learner’s role. · That concerns a question establishing what support is actually needed, rather than seeking a responsible adult or trained support for a concern beyond the learner’s role. · That concerns wording that preserves a person’s dignity, rather than seeking a responsible adult or trained support for a concern beyond the learner’s role. | Language practice does not supply clinical training or authority. |

Right answer: `{X.r}`; wrong choices: `{X.w[0]}`, `{X.w[1]}`, `{X.w[2]}`

- Feedback `w0`: “{X.e[0]}”
- Feedback `w1`: “{X.e[1]}”
- Feedback `w2`: “{X.e[2]}”

Three generated variants:

> Which term means wording that preserves a person’s dignity?

- ◻️ polite offer  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns a courteous proposal of assistance that leaves a choice, rather than wording that preserves a person’s dignity._
- ✅ respectful language
- ◻️ clarifying question  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns a question establishing what support is actually needed, rather than wording that preserves a person’s dignity._
- ◻️ appropriate referral  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns seeking a responsible adult or trained support for a concern beyond the learner’s role, rather than wording that preserves a person’s dignity._

> So the answer is “respectful language”.

- ◻️ clarifying question  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns a question establishing what support is actually needed, rather than seeking a responsible adult or trained support for a concern beyond the learner’s role._
- ◻️ respectful language  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns wording that preserves a person’s dignity, rather than seeking a responsible adult or trained support for a concern beyond the learner’s role._
- ◻️ polite offer  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns a courteous proposal of assistance that leaves a choice, rather than seeking a responsible adult or trained support for a concern beyond the learner’s role._

### `e4-2-build` · Lesson 2 · word order, level 2

Prompt: Tap the words in the right order.

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| a | b |
|---|---|
| Could you explain the proposal, please? | Could you explain the proposal, please? |
| I understand your point, but I have a question. | I understand your point, but I have a question. |

### `e4-2-repair` · Lesson 2 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the incorrect claim or language form.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ polite offer: a courteous proposal of assistance that leaves a choice.
- ✅ clarifying question: a question establishing what support is actually needed.
- ✅ respectful language: wording that preserves a person’s dignity.
- ✅ appropriate referral: seeking a responsible adult or trained support for a concern beyond the learner’s role.
- ❌ An offer means ignoring every refusal.

### `e4-4-terms` · Lesson 4 · multiple choice, level 1

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Which term means a repeated activity described with the simple present? | present habit | completed past event · past negative · present agreement | That concerns an action presented as finished in its past context, rather than a repeated activity described with the simple present. · That concerns did not followed by the base verb for an ordinary verb, rather than a repeated activity described with the simple present. · That concerns the third-person singular form with an appropriate subject, rather than a repeated activity described with the simple present. | The simple present can report a repeated activity. |
| Which term means an action presented as finished in its past context? | completed past event | present habit · past negative · present agreement | That concerns a repeated activity described with the simple present, rather than an action presented as finished in its past context. · That concerns did not followed by the base verb for an ordinary verb, rather than an action presented as finished in its past context. · That concerns the third-person singular form with an appropriate subject, rather than an action presented as finished in its past context. | Use the simple past for the completed event in this model. |
| Which term means did not followed by the base verb for an ordinary verb? | past negative | present habit · completed past event · present agreement | That concerns a repeated activity described with the simple present, rather than did not followed by the base verb for an ordinary verb. · That concerns an action presented as finished in its past context, rather than did not followed by the base verb for an ordinary verb. · That concerns the third-person singular form with an appropriate subject, rather than did not followed by the base verb for an ordinary verb. | Did carries the past; the following verb stays in the base form. |
| Which term means the third-person singular form with an appropriate subject? | present agreement | present habit · completed past event · past negative | That concerns a repeated activity described with the simple present, rather than the third-person singular form with an appropriate subject. · That concerns an action presented as finished in its past context, rather than the third-person singular form with an appropriate subject. · That concerns did not followed by the base verb for an ordinary verb, rather than the third-person singular form with an appropriate subject. | The singular third-person subject requires the appropriate present form. |

### `e4-4-repair-pattern` · Lesson 4 · multiple choice, level 2

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Correct the past negative. | Bih did not go. | Bih did not went. · Bih do not went. · Bih not went. | After did use go. · The auxiliary and lexical form are wrong for this past negative. · An ordinary past negative needs did not in this model. | Did not takes the base go. |
| Correct the question about a past purchase. | Did Ayuk buy it? | Did Ayuk bought it? · Does Ayuk bought it? · Did Ayuk buys it? | Use buy after did. · This mixes present auxiliary with a past form. · Do not add third-person -s after did. | Did carries the past in the question. |
| Choose the ordinary present habit with she. | She reads after chores. | She read every evening, in the stated present habit. · She reading after chores. · She do reads after chores. | The explicit present-habit context needs third-person agreement. · The phrase lacks a finite verb. · After do the ordinary form would be base; this is not the requested neutral habit pattern. | She takes reads in this model. |
| Choose the finished event in the stated past context. | They arrived yesterday. | They arrives yesterday. · They arriving yesterday. · They did arrived yesterday. | That present singular form does not fit. · A finite verb is missing. · Did would take arrive, not arrived. | Arrived is a finite simple-past form. |

Right answer: `{X.r}`; wrong choices: `{X.w[0]}`, `{X.w[1]}`, `{X.w[2]}`

- Feedback `w0`: “{X.e[0]}”
- Feedback `w1`: “{X.e[1]}”
- Feedback `w2`: “{X.e[2]}”

Three generated variants:

> Choose the finished event in the stated past context.

- ✅ They arrived yesterday.
- ◻️ They did arrived yesterday.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Did would take arrive, not arrived._
- ◻️ They arriving yesterday.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: A finite verb is missing._
- ◻️ They arrives yesterday.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That present singular form does not fit._

Full working after a second miss:

> Arrived is a finite simple-past form.  
> So the answer is “They arrived yesterday.”.  

> Correct the past negative.

- ◻️ Bih do not went.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: The auxiliary and lexical form are wrong for this past negative._
- ◻️ Bih not went.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: An ordinary past negative needs did not in this model._
- ◻️ Bih did not went.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: After did use go._
- ✅ Bih did not go.

Full working after a second miss:

> Did not takes the base go.  
> So the answer is “Bih did not go.”.  

> Choose the ordinary present habit with she.

- ◻️ She read every evening, in the stated present habit.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: The explicit present-habit context needs third-person agreement._
- ✅ She reads after chores.
- ◻️ She do reads after chores.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: After do the ordinary form would be base; this is not the requested neutral habit pattern._
- ◻️ She reading after chores.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: The phrase lacks a finite verb._

Full working after a second miss:

> She takes reads in this model.  
> So the answer is “She reads after chores.”.

### `e4-6-repair` · Lesson 6 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the incorrect claim or language form.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ explicit detail: information directly stated in the passage.
- ✅ supported inference: an interpretation linked to clues in the passage.
- ✅ main idea: the central message organising the passage.
- ✅ information not supplied: a detail the passage does not allow the reader to determine.
- ❌ An explicit detail is always something invented by the reader.  
  _↳ Direct information appears in the passage._
- ❌ Any private guess is a proven inference.  
  _↳ An inference needs textual support._
- ❌ A random minor word always gives the complete main idea.  
  _↳ The main idea must represent the relevant whole._
- ❌ An absent detail must always be replaced with a guessed number.  
  _↳ A faithful answer acknowledges missing information._

### `e4-15-terms` · Lesson 15 · multiple choice, level 1

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |

### `e4-30-repair` · Lesson 30 · spot the error, level 3

- ✅ qualified proposal: a suggested action recognising relevant conditions or limits.
- ❌ Reduction necessarily means producing more waste.  
  _↳ The aim is to prevent part of the use or waste._

  _↳ The object can remain intact while its use changes._

  _↳ Specific conditions make a proposal more realistic._

- ✅ main idea: the central message organising the passage.
- ✅ information not supplied: a detail the passage does not allow the reader to determine.
- ❌ An explicit detail is always something invented by the reader.  
  _↳ Direct information appears in the passage._
- ❌ Any private guess is a proven inference.  
  _↳ An inference needs textual support._
